=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
We have a long, thin piece of paper whose thickness can be ignored. We perform the following operation 100 times: lift the right end, fold it so that it aligns with the left end using the center as a crease. After completing the 100 folds, we unfold the paper back to its original state. At this point, there are 2^{100} - 1 creases on the paper, and these creases can be classified into two types: mountain folds and valley folds. The figure below represents the state after performing the operation twice, where red solid lines represent mountain folds and red dashed lines represent valley folds.

About mountain and valley folds

- A crease is a mountain fold if it is folded so that the back sides of the paper come together at the crease.
- A crease is a valley fold if it is folded so that the front sides of the paper come together at the crease.



You are given a sequence A = (A_1, A_2, \dots, A_N) of N non-negative integers. Here, 0 = A_1 < A_2 < \dots < A_N \leq 10^{18}.
For each integer i from 1 through 2^{100} - A_N - 1, define f(i) as follows:

- The number of k = 1, 2, \dots, N such that the (i + A_k)-th crease from the left is a mountain fold.

Find the maximum value among f(1), f(2), \dots, f(2^{100} - A_N - 1).

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
A_1 A_2 \cdots A_N

Output

Print the answer in one line.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 10^3
- 0 = A_1 < A_2 < \dots < A_N \leq 10^{18}

Sample Input 1

4
0 1 2 3

Sample Output 1

3

If mountain and valley folds are represented by M and V, respectively, there is a contiguous subsequence of creases like MMVM. There is no contiguous subsequence like MMMM, so the answer is 3.

Sample Input 2

6
0 2 3 5 7 8

Sample Output 2

4

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Maybe use automaton over binary representation. P(n) can be computed by reading binary from LSB? Since P(n)=1 iff when n is written as ...? Let's derive.

Let n = 2^a * m, m odd. m≡1 mod4 iff the binary representation of m ends with "01" (since odd m mod4 = 1 if last two bits 01). Thus P(n)=1 iff after stripping trailing zeros, the last two bits are 01. Equivalently, the binary representation of n ends with "01" followed by some number of zeros? Wait n = m * 2^a. Case x = 2u+1 (odd). For each a:
- If a even, a=2b: P(2u+1+2b)=P(2(u+b)+1)= [u+b even].
- If a odd, a=2b+1: P(2u+1+2b+1)=P(2(u+b+1))=P(u+b+1).

This recurrence reduces argument u by about half, but introduces parity conditions for the opposite parity offsets. We need maximize over x in a huge interval. We can recursively branch on parity of x. Depth up to 100 (because L=2^100). At each level, the set of offsets transforms: even offsets halve and go into P term; odd offsets become parity conditions depending on u. The parity conditions are simple: count of offsets of a certain parity satisfying u + c even. That is a function of u mod2. So at each recursive step, the count can be decomposed into:
C(x; A) = C(floor(x/2); A_even') + parity_count(floor(x/2) mod2; A_odd')
depending on parity of x, where A_even' are halved even offsets for P term, and A_odd' are constants for parity term.

But note the parity term depends only on u mod2, not on higher bits. The P term involves a new set of offsets (halved even offsets) and argument u. Thus we can recursively maximize over u. This is like a binary decision tree of depth 100, with state being the set of offsets transformed by repeatedly taking even offsets and halving, plus accumulated parity counts from odd offsets at each level. However, at each step, only even offsets survive to next P term; odd offsets are "consumed" into a parity contribution depending on current u's parity. Since A_N <=1e18, after about 60 halvings, all offsets become 0? Actually if we repeatedly take even offsets and halve, offsets decrease. Odd offsets are removed from the P-term set and contribute parity conditions. After enough steps, the set may become empty or contain only 0? Let's see.

Starting with offsets A including 0. At each recursion, for the P term we use only even offsets, halved. Odd offsets are removed. Thus the number of offsets in the recursive P-term set is nonincreasing; after at most max v2? Actually an offset a survives t levels iff a divisible by 2^t; then becomes a/2^t. So after t > max v2(A) (<=60), no offsets survive except maybe 0? Wait 0 is even and survives forever: 0/2=0. Since A1=0, the P-term set always contains 0. Other offsets eventually become odd and are removed. So after about 60 levels, the P-term set is just {0}. Then C(x; {0}) = P(x). But we also have accumulated parity contributions from all removed offsets at various levels. This suggests we can compute C(x) by processing bits of x from LSB upward: each offset a contributes either P(x+a) directly, but via recurrence, as we peel off bits of x, each offset contributes at the level where its transformed value becomes odd (or at final if 0). Specifically, for a fixed a and x, consider binary addition? The recurrence essentially computes P(x+a) by looking at the first bit position where x and a? Let's derive.

For a fixed a, P(x+a) can be computed by the recurrence:
Let x parity p = x mod2.
If p=0:
- if a even: P(x+a)=P(x/2 + a/2)
- if a odd: P(x+a)= [x/2 + (a-1)/2 even] = 1 if parity of x/2 equals parity of (a-1)/2? Actually u+b even means u mod2 = b mod2? Since b=(a-1)/2. So condition u ≡ b (mod2).
If p=1:
- if a even: P(x+a)= [ (x-1)/2 + a/2 even ] = parity condition.
- if a odd: P(x+a)=P((x-1)/2 + (a+1)/2).

So if the parity of x matches parity of a (both even or both odd), the P term recurses with floor(x/2) plus adjusted a. If parities differ, the value is determined by a parity condition at this bit (and no further recursion for that offset). This is intuitive: P(n) depends on the first bit (LSB upward) where n has a 1? Let's see.

When adding x and a, the low bit of sum is p xor a0. The recurrence peels off bits. If the sum's low bit is 0 (i.e., x and a same parity), then P(sum) = P(sum/2): we can divide by 2 and continue. If the sum's low bit is 1 (x and a different parity), then P(sum) is determined by the next bit of sum/2? Actually P(2n+1) = 1 if n even. So if sum is odd, P depends on parity of n = (sum-1)/2, i.e., the second bit of sum. This is exactly determined by the carry into the second bit, which depends on x/2 + ... parity. So once the sum becomes odd at some bit, P is determined by the next bit; no further recursion.

Thus for each offset a, P(x+a) is determined by the first bit position t where the sum x+a has a 1? More precisely, let s = x+a. If v2(s)=t, then s is even for bits <t, and bit t=1. P(s)=1 iff bit t+1=0. The recurrence peels off t zeros (when sum even) by dividing by 2; at step t, sum becomes odd, then P is determined by parity of the halved sum, i.e., bit t+1. So yes.

Thus the recursive process:

- For each a in A:

- The next argument y' = (y+p)/2 has parity q = y' mod2, which is the next bit of the original x sequence.

Comparison update:
def update_cmp(cmp, bit_val, a_bit):
    if bit_val > a_bit: return GREATER
    if bit_val < a_bit: return LESS
    return cmp
Where cmp is 0 EQUAL, 1 GREATER, 2 LESS. Since we process LSB to MSB, later bits override. This is correct.

Let's test comparison with A_N=3 (binary11), z=4 (100). Process bits LSB:
cmp=EQUAL.
t0 z0=0, a0=1 -> LESS.
t1 z1=0, a1=1 -> LESS.
t2 z2=1, a2=0 -> GREATER (overrides). Final GREATER. z=4>3. Good.
z=2 (010): t0 0 vs1 LESS; t1 1 vs1 equal -> LESS; t2 0 vs0 equal -> LESS. Final LESS. Good.
z=3: t0 1 vs1 eq; t1 1 vs1 eq; t2 0 vs0 eq -> EQUAL. z=3 not >3, invalid. Good.

Now, reward_func uses get_child. Need ensure cnt for empty nodes is 0. For real nodes, cnt is number of offsets in subtree. For root, cnt=N. For nodes after depth60 high chain, cnt=N (all offsets) because all have zeros. But wait at depth60, there are multiple nodes each with cnt maybe1. Their child0 all point to the same high chain node with cnt=N? Is that correct? If a node at depth60 represents a specific low60 prefix, its child0 should represent offsets with that prefix and bit60=0. Since all offsets have bit60=0, the child0 contains exactly the offsets in that node, not all N. If we merge child0 across different depth60 nodes, we would incorrectly include offsets with different low60 prefixes. So we cannot merge high chain across different nodes! Important.

Let's think: Trie nodes are defined by prefix bits. A node at depth d represents all offsets whose low d bits equal that prefix. If two different nodes at depth60 have different prefixes, their child0 nodes at depth61 have prefixes differing in the first 60 bits, so they are different sets. Even though all offsets have bit60=0, the child0 node for prefix P contains offsets with low61 bits = P + 0, which is a subset of P. Different P yield disjoint subsets. Therefore they cannot be merged into a single node. My earlier thought that all offsets share high zeros after depth60 is false because the prefix includes the low60 bits; the high zero chain is shared only if the low60 prefix is the same. Since each offset has a unique low60 prefix, after depth60 each offset follows its own zero chain; these chains are distinct. Thus total nodes after depth60: N * (MAX_DEPTH-60) if we insert each offset separately, because each offset's path is unique from root (low bits) and then zeros. Since low60 bits distinguish all offsets, no sharing after depth60. So nodes ~ N*102 = 102k, still fine. Good catch.

When inserting, if a node at depth60 already has child0 from a previous offset with same low60 prefix? But low60 prefix unique, so no sharing. For offset 0, path all zeros; for offset 1, path bit0=1 then zeros; they diverge at bit0. So high chains are separate. Total nodes <= N*102 = 102k.

Now, reward_func for t>=60: resolved_node = child[node][1-p]. Since offsets have bit_t=0 for t>=60, if p=1, resolved_node = child[node][0] which contains all offsets in node (because all have bit_t=0). If p=0, resolved_node = child[node][1] empty. The reward then counts child[resolved_node][need_bit]. For t>=60, offsets have bit_{t+1}=0, so if need_bit=0, reward=cnt(resolved_node); if need_bit=1, reward=0. Our trie with separate chains will have child[resolved_node][0] containing the same offsets (since bit_{t+1}=0), so cnt correct. Good.

Now, building trie to depth MAX_DEPTH=102 for each offset: For a=0, path all zeros. For a with bit_length 60, after bit59, bits 60..101 zero. So each offset creates a chain of length 102. Nodes total N*102 = 102k, okay.

But note if two offsets share a long prefix of low bits, they share nodes. Since N=1000, worst-case 102k.

Define MAX_DEPTH = 102 (we need nodes up to depth101, plus maybe 102). Let's set MAX_DEPTH = 102. Insert bits 0..MAX_DEPTH-1 (0..101). So nodes up to depth102. For t=99, reward needs child depth101; available. For final cnt, child depth100. Good.

Node class:
class Node:
    __slots__ = ('depth','cnt','child0','child1')
    def __init__(self, depth):
        self.depth = depth
        self.cnt = 0
        self.child0 = None
        self.child1 = None

empty_nodes = [Node(i) for i in range(MAX_DEPTH+1)]  # depth 0..MAX_DEPTH
For empty nodes, cnt=0, children None.

get_child(node, bit):
    if node is None:
        # Should not happen if we always pass Node; but for safety return empty_nodes[0]? Need depth.
        return empty_nodes[0]
    if bit == 0:
        ch = node.child0
    else:
        ch = node.child1
    if ch is not None:
        return ch
    # if node is empty or missing child, return empty node at next depth
    if node.depth < MAX_DEPTH:
        return empty_nodes[node.depth + 1]
    else:
        return empty_nodes[MAX_DEPTH]

But if node is a real node and missing child, returning empty_nodes[depth+1] is correct. If node is empty, child0/child1 None, returns empty_nodes[depth+1]. Good.

Insert:
root = Node(0)
for a in A:
    node = root
    for t in range(MAX_DEPTH):
        node.cnt += 1
        bit = (a >> t) & 1
        if bit == 0:
            if node.child0 is None:
                node.child0 = Node(t+1)
            node = node.child0
        else:
            if node.child1 is None:
                node.child1 = Node(t+1)
            node = node.child1
    node.cnt += 1
After inserting all, root.cnt=N.

Define INF = -10**9.

def solve(node, p, cmp_state):
    t = node.depth
    if t == 99:
        # choose p100=1
        reward = calc_reward(node, p, 1)
        next_node = get_child(node, p)
        total = reward + next_node.cnt
        if cmp_state == GREATER:
            return total
        else:
            return -INF
    best = -INF
    for c in (0,1):
        reward = calc_reward(node, p, c)
        next_node = get_child(node, p)  # same for both c
        new_cmp = update_cmp(cmp_state, c, (A_N >> (t+1)) & 1)
        val = reward + solve(next_node, c, new_cmp)
        if val > best:
            best = val
    return best

Wait: next_node = get_child(node, p) is independent of c, so we can compute once. But solve(next_node, c, new_cmp) depends on c. Good.

But is next_node independent of c? Yes, v_{t+1} = child[v_t][p_t] = child[node][p]. It does not depend on p_{t+1}. So compute once.

However, in the transition, the reward R_t uses c, and then the next state's node is v_{t+1}. This means the DP from state (node,p) branches on c only through reward and cmp, not through node. This is a bit surprising but correct. Let's verify with the earlier state definition: At time t, we have chosen p_t=p. The node v_t is depth t. The next node v_{t+1} is determined by p_t, not p_{t+1}. So yes.

But wait, in the trie, v_t is the set of offsets that survived t steps, i.e., low t bits match p_0..p_{t-1}. To survive to t+1, an offset must additionally have bit_t = p_t. So v_{t+1} = child[v_t][p_t]. This is independent of p_{t+1}. Correct.

Now, calc_reward(node, p, c):
if node.cnt == 0: return 0
resolved = get_child(node, 1-p)
if resolved.cnt == 0: return 0
need = c ^ p
return get_child(resolved, need).cnt

Now, calc_reward:
def calc_reward(node, p, c):
    if node.cnt == 0:
        return 0
    resolved = get_child(node, 1-p)
    if resolved.cnt == 0:
        return 0
    need = c ^ p
    return get_child(resolved, need).cnt

What about p_t for t>100? Not needed because all offsets resolve by t=99? Wait if x+a has v2=99, reward at t=99 uses p100. If v2 could be 100? No. So p101 not needed. Good.

But what if x+a = 0? No.

Now, let's prove that all offsets resolve by t=99 for valid x. Since x+a <2^100 and >0, v2(x+a) <=99. The recurrence resolves an offset at step t = v2(x+a). So t<=99. Thus after processing t=99, no survivors. Our final cnt should be0. But if due to z>A_N, v_100 empty. Let's prove: v_100 contains offsets a with a ≡ z mod2^100. Since 0<=a<=A_N < z (because z>A_N), a != z. Also a and z are both in [0,2^100-1], so congruence mod2^100 implies equality. Thus empty. Good.

Now, what about A_N=0, z>0, a=0, z>0 so not equal. Empty.

Thus final cnt always0. We can omit final cnt to simplify, but keep.

Now, let's think about the comparison state in solve for empty nodes. If node is empty at depth t, the remaining bits can be chosen arbitrarily to satisfy z>A_N. The solve will explore all c values and cmp updates. Since empty nodes per depth are few, fine.

Now, potential optimization: In solve, next_node = get_child(node, p) is independent of c. We can compute once. But if node is empty, get_child returns empty_node[t+1]. Good.

Now, let's write the code.

Pseudo:

import sys
from functools import lru_cache

MAX_DEPTH = 102
EQUAL, GREATER, LESS = 0,1,2

class Node:
    __slots__ = ('depth','cnt','child0','child1')
    def __init__(self, depth):
        self.depth = depth
        self.cnt = 0
        self.child0 = None
        self.child1 = None

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.readline
    N = int(input())
    A = list(map(int, input().split()))
    A_N = A[-1]

    empty_nodes = [Node(i) for i in range(MAX_DEPTH+1)]

    def get_child(node, bit):
        if bit == 0:
            ch = node.child0
        else:
            ch = node.child1
        if ch is not None:
            return ch
        if node.depth < MAX_DEPTH:
            return empty_nodes[node.depth + 1]
        return empty_nodes[MAX_DEPTH]

    root = Node(0)
    for a in A:
        node = root
        for t in range(MAX_DEPTH):
            node.cnt += 1
            bit = (a >> t) & 1
            if bit == 0:
                if node.child0 is None:
                    node.child0 = Node(t+1)
                node = node.child0
            else:
                if node.child1 is None:
                    node.child1 = Node(t+1)
                node = node.child1
        node.cnt += 1

    def update_cmp(cmp_state, bit, abit):
        if bit > abit:
            return GREATER
        if bit < abit:
            return LESS
        return cmp_state

    def calc_reward(node, p, c):
        if node.cnt == 0:
            return 0
        resolved = get_child(node, 1-p)
        if resolved.cnt == 0:
            return 0
        need = c ^ p
        return get_child(resolved, need).cnt

    @lru_cache(maxsize=None)
    def solve(node, p, cmp_state):
        t = node.depth
        if t == 99:
            reward = calc_reward(node, p, 1)
            next_node = get_child(node, p)
            total = reward + next_node.cnt
            return total if cmp_state == GREATER else -10**9
        best = -10**9
        next_node = get_child(node, p)
        # c can be 0 or1
        for c in (0,1):
            reward = calc_reward(node, p, c)
            new_cmp = update_cmp(cmp_state, c, (A_N >> (t+1)) & 1)
            val = reward + solve(next_node, c, new_cmp)
            if val > best:
                best = val
        return best

    ans = -10**9
    for p0 in (0,1):
        new_cmp = update_cmp(EQUAL, p0, A_N & 1)
        val = solve(root, p0, new_cmp)
        if val > ans:
            ans = val
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Need check MAX_DEPTH: We insert bits 0..MAX_DEPTH-1. If MAX_DEPTH=102, nodes up to depth102. empty_nodes length 103 (0..102). In get_child, if node.depth < MAX_DEPTH, return empty_nodes[node.depth+1]. If node.depth=102, return empty_nodes[102]. But get_child on depth102 might be called? In solve, t=node.depth. For t=99, next_node=get_child(node,p) where node.depth=99 -> depth100. calc_reward for t=99: resolved=get_child(node,1-p) depth100; get_child(resolved, need) depth101. No get_child on depth102. For t=98, next_node depth99; calc_reward get_child(resolved, need) depth100. So max get_child depth101. So MAX_DEPTH=102 is safe. But in insertion, we create nodes up to depth102. Fine.

However, in solve, if node is empty at depth t, next_node=get_child(node,p) returns empty_nodes[t+1]. For t=99, next_node depth100. calc_reward node empty returns0. Good.

One issue: The root node is real, but empty_nodes[0] also exists. We never use empty_nodes[0] as root. get_child on a real node missing child returns empty_nodes[depth+1]. For depth0, returns empty_nodes[1]. Good.

Now, let's verify the reward formula once more with the paperfolding recurrence.

Given P(2n)=P(n), P(2n+1)=1 if n even else0.

At step t, for an offset in node v_t, let s_t = (x+a)/2^t. We know s_t is integer. Let p = p_t = bit_t(-x) = s_t mod2? Is s_t mod2 equal to p_t? Since x+a divisible by2^t, s_t = (x+a)/2^t. Mod2, s_t ≡ (x+a)/2^t mod2. We know a ≡ z mod2^t, so a = z_low + 2^t b. x = 2^100 - z. For t<100, x/2^t? Hmm. But the recurrence state y_t = ceil(x/2^t) and b_t = floor(a/2^t) gave s_t = y_t + b_t. The parity of y_t is p_t. The parity of b_t is bit_t(a). Thus s_t mod2 = p_t xor bit_t(a). If bit_t(a) != p_t, s_t odd. Then P(s_t) = P(2u+1) where u=(s_t-1)/2. P=1 iff u even. u = (s_t-1)/2. We need express u parity in terms of p_{t+1} and bit_{t+1}(a). Since s_t = y_t + b_t. If s_t odd, then y_t and b_t have opposite parity. Let p = y_t mod2, a_bit = b_t mod2 =1-p. Write y_t = 2u_y + p, b_t = 2u_b + (1-p). Then s_t = 2(u_y+u_b) +1. u = u_y+u_b. P=1 iff u_y+u_b even.
Now y_{t+1} = (y_t + p)/2 = u_y + p. Wait earlier y_{t+1} = (y_t + p)/2 = u_y + p? Since y_t=2u_y+p, (y_t+p)/2 = u_y+p. But p is 0/1. Actually if p=1, (2u_y+1+1)/2 = u_y+1. So y_{t+1} = u_y + p. Its parity p_{t+1} = (u_y + p) mod2 = u_y mod2 xor p.
b_{t+1} = floor(b_t/2) = u_b. Its parity is bit_{t+1}(a) = u_b mod2.
We need u_y + u_b even => u_y mod2 = u_b mod2.
p_{t+1} = u_y mod2 xor p. So u_y mod2 = p_{t+1} xor p.
Thus condition u_y = u_b => p_{t+1} xor p = bit_{t+1}(a). => p_{t+1} = bit_{t+1}(a) xor p. This matches our reward condition: value1 iff p_{t+1} = bit_{t+1}(a) xor p_t. In calc_reward, need = c xor p, and we count resolved offsets with bit_{t+1}=need. Since value1 iff bit_{t+1}=need. Good.

If bit_t(a) == p_t, s_t even, P(s_t)=P(s_t/2)=P(y_{t+1}+b_{t+1}), so offset survives. Good.

Thus DP exact.

Now, let's think about potential bug in update_cmp: We process bits from LSB to MSB. The comparison state update "if bit > abit: GREATER; if bit < abit: LESS; else unchanged" is correct because later bits are more significant. Let's prove with an example where low bits differ but high bits same: z=2 (010), A=1 (001). LSB: z0=0, a0=1 -> LESS. bit1: z1=1, a1=0 -> GREATER (overrides). Final GREATER, z>A. Good. If high bits same, low difference remains. Good.

We can implement bottom-up DP per depth to be safe and avoid lru_cache memory overhead. Let's see.

We need compute dp for states (node, p, cmp) for depths 0..99. Since transition from t to t+1 uses next_node = child[node][p] and c. We can store dp as a dictionary mapping (node_id, p, cmp) to value for current depth, and compute previous depth. But nodes are organized by depth. We can create a list of nodes_by_depth: for each depth, list of nodes (real + empty). Then dp[d] as a 3D array? Number of nodes per depth varies. We can use dictionaries.

Simpler: Use lru_cache. Given constraints small, it's fine. But to be robust, we can implement iterative with dictionaries:

- Build list nodes_by_depth: for depth 0..99, include all real nodes at that depth and empty_nodes[depth]. But real nodes at depth >99 not needed for solve states. We need nodes up to99 for states; nodes depth100/101 only for counts.
- dp_next for depth 100? Actually base at t=99 uses reward and final cnt, no recursion. We can compute dp for t=99 directly for all states (node at depth99, p, cmp). Then for t=98 down to0, compute dp_t from dp_{t+1}.

But the transition from t to t+1 uses next_node = child[node][p] which has depth t+1. The next state's current bit is c. So dp_t[node][p][cmp] = max_c reward(node,p,c) + dp_{t+1}[next_node][c][new_cmp]. This is easy.

We need dp_{t+1} for all nodes at depth t+1, including empty_nodes[t+1]. We can store as a dictionary keyed by (id(node), p, cmp) or a 3D list if we assign indices.

Since total states ~600k, dictionaries okay. But lru_cache simpler.

Let's estimate lru_cache memory: Each cache entry key is a tuple of 3 objects. 600k entries * ~100 bytes =60MB plus dict overhead maybe 100MB. Acceptable. But AtCoder Python memory limit often 1024MB, okay. If memory limit 256MB, maybe tight. We can reduce by using iterative DP with arrays to be safer.

Let's consider implementing iterative DP with dictionaries per depth to reduce memory. We only need dp for next depth when computing current. But the transition from t to t+1 uses dp_{t+1} for next_node and c. So we can compute backwards, storing dp for depth t+1 as a dict. Then compute dp for depth t and discard t+1. This uses memory O(states per depth) not total. The number of states per depth: nodes at that depth *2*3. Nodes at depth up to60 maybe up to N*? At depth d, nodes <= min(2^d, N*(d+1)?). Worst-case N=1000, depth60 nodes 1000? Actually each offset path diverges; at depth60 there are up to1000 nodes. At depth0 one. So per depth <=1000 real nodes +1 empty. States per depth <=1001*6=6006. Very small! Wait total nodes 102k but distributed over 102 depths, average 1000. So per depth ~6000 states. Iterative DP memory tiny. This is better.

Let's implement iterative DP.

We need nodes_by_depth for depths 0..99 (states). We can build a list of lists: nodes_at_depth[d] = [empty_nodes[d]] + all real nodes with depth d. But we also need for reward get_child to nodes depth d+1/d+2; those are accessible via child pointers, no need list.

We need dp for depth 100? Actually base t=99 computed directly. We can compute dp[99] as a dict mapping (node_id, p, cmp) -> value. Then for t from98 down to0, compute dp_t.

But in transition, dp_{t+1} is keyed by next_node (depth t+1), c, new_cmp. So we need a function get_dp(dp_dict, node, p, cmp) returning value or -INF if not present. For each state at depth t, we compute.

State space at depth t: node in nodes_at_depth[t], p in {0,1}, cmp in {0,1,2}. We can iterate.

Base t=99:
For each node in nodes_at_depth[99], p, cmp:
    reward = calc_reward(node, p, 1)
    next_node = get_child(node, p)
    total = reward + next_node.cnt
    dp[ (id(node), p, cmp) ] = total if cmp == GREATER else -INF
But if node is empty, reward0, next_node empty cnt0, total0 if cmp GREATER else -INF.

Then for t from98 down to0:
    new_dp = {}
    for node in nodes_at_depth[t]:
        for p in (0,1):
            next_node = get_child(node, p)  # depth t+1
            for cmp_state in (0,1,2):
                best = -INF
                for c in (0,1):
                    reward = calc_reward(node, p, c)
                    new_cmp = update_cmp(cmp_state, c, (A_N >> (t+1)) & 1)
                    val = reward + dp.get((id(next_node), c, new_cmp), -INF)
                    if val > best: best = val
                new_dp[(id(node), p, cmp_state)] = best
    dp = new_dp

At the end, answer = max over p0, cmp0 of dp[(id(root), p0, update_cmp(EQUAL,p0,A_N&1))]? Wait after loop t=0, dp corresponds to depth0 states. Yes. We can compute:
ans = -INF
for p0 in (0,1):
    cmp0 = update_cmp(EQUAL, p0, A_N & 1)
    ans = max(ans, dp.get((id(root), p0, cmp0), -INF))
print(ans)

This iterative DP avoids recursion and lru_cache. Need ensure nodes_at_depth includes empty_nodes[d] for d=0..99. For d=0, empty_nodes[0] is not root; but state at depth0 only root? Could there be a state with empty_nodes[0]? No, start at root. But for uniformity, we can include empty_nodes[0] in nodes_at_depth[0]; it won't be used except maybe if root child missing? At depth0, state node is always root. But including empty is fine. However, root is real depth0. nodes_at_depth[0] = [root] + [empty_nodes[0]]? We don't need empty at depth0 because no transition leads to depth0 empty. But harmless. For depth>0, empty_nodes[d] may be reached when a real node lacks a child. So include empty_nodes[d] for all d.

We need build nodes_at_depth. During insertion, we can append new nodes to nodes_at_depth[depth]. But if we create empty_nodes separately, add them. Let's do:

for d in range(MAX_DEPTH+1):
    nodes_at_depth[d].append(empty_nodes[d])
When creating a real Node(depth), append to nodes_at_depth[depth].

But note we insert to depth102; nodes_at_depth length 103. For DP states we only need depths 0..99. But nodes_at_depth[100],[101] used for counts, not states. We can still store.

Potential issue: In the transition, for a given state (node,p,cmp), next_node = get_child(node,p) is independent of c. But if node is empty, get_child returns empty_nodes[t+1]. Good.

Let's implement iterative DP with dictionaries.

Pseudo code:

MAX_DEPTH = 101

class Node:
    __slots__ = ('depth','cnt','child0','child1')
    ...

empty_nodes = [Node(i) for i in range(MAX_DEPTH+1)]
nodes_at_depth = [[] for _ in range(MAX_DEPTH+1)]
for d in range(MAX_DEPTH+1):
    nodes_at_depth[d].append(empty_nodes[d])

root = Node(0); nodes_at_depth[0].append(root)  # but root created before? We'll create root after empty? Need append.

Insertion:
for a in A:
    node = root
    for t in range(MAX_DEPTH):
        node.cnt += 1
        bit = (a >> t) & 1
        if bit == 0:
            if node.child0 is None:
                node.child0 = Node(t+1)
                nodes_at_depth[t+1].append(node.child0)
            node = node.child0
        else:
            if node.child1 is None:
                node.child1 = Node(t+1)
                nodes_at_depth[t+1].append(node.child1)
            node = node.child1
    node.cnt += 1

But root is created before nodes_at_depth? We'll create root = Node(0) and append to nodes_at_depth[0]. But empty_nodes[0] also in nodes_at_depth[0]. Fine.

However, if we create root before empty_nodes? Doesn't matter. Let's create empty_nodes first, then root.

Now, get_child uses empty_nodes. For a real node missing child, returns empty_nodes[depth+1]. But note if a real node at depth d has child missing, and later another offset insertion would create that child, we set node.child0 = Node. But if we previously returned empty_nodes for that missing child in DP? DP runs after all insertions, so no issue.

Now, calc_reward:
def calc_reward(node, p, c):
    if node.cnt == 0:
        return 0
    resolved = get_child(node, 1-p)
    if resolved.cnt == 0:
        return 0
    need = c ^ p
    return get_child(resolved, need).cnt

Now, DP base:
dp = {}
for node in nodes_at_depth[99]:
    for p in (0,1):
        for cmp_state in (0,1,2):
            reward = calc_reward(node, p, 1)
            next_node = get_child(node, p)
            total = reward + next_node.cnt
            dp[(node, p, cmp_state)] = total if cmp_state == GREATER else -INF

Then for t in range(98, -1, -1):
    new_dp = {}
    for node in nodes_at_depth[t]:
        for p in (0,1):
            next_node = get_child(node, p)
            for cmp_state in (0,1,2):
                best = -INF
                for c in (0,1):
                    reward = calc_reward(node, p, c)
                    new_cmp = update_cmp(cmp_state, c, (A_N >> (t+1)) & 1)
                    val = reward + dp.get((next_node, c, new_cmp), -INF)
                    if val > best:
                        best = val
                new_dp[(node, p, cmp_state)] = best
    dp = new_dp

At the end:
ans = -INF
for p0 in (0,1):
    cmp0 = update_cmp(EQUAL, p0, A_N & 1)
    val = dp.get((root, p0, cmp0), -INF)
    if val > ans: ans = val
print(ans)

But wait: In the transition, dp.get((next_node, c, new_cmp), -INF). dp is for depth t+1. next_node is depth t+1. Good. But what if next_node is a real node at depth t+1 that is not in nodes_at_depth[t+1]? It is, because we appended all created nodes. If next_node is empty_nodes[t+1], it's in nodes_at_depth. Good.

Now, in base t=99, dp includes states for all nodes at depth99. But in transition from t=98, next_node could be a real node at depth99 or empty_nodes[99]. Both in dp. Good.

Now, let's think about the comparison update in the transition: We update cmp with bit t+1 of z, which is c. But c is p_{t+1}. Yes. For t=98, update bit99. Then dp for depth99 has cmp after bit99. Base t=99 uses that cmp. Good.

Full code:

import sys

MAX_DEPTH = 101
EQUAL, GREATER, LESS = 0,1,2
INF = -10**9

class Node:
    __slots__ = ('depth','cnt','child0','child1')
    def __init__(self, depth):
        self.depth = depth
        self.cnt = 0
        self.child0 = None
        self.child1 = None

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:1+N]
    A_N = A[-1]

    empty_nodes = [Node(i) for i in range(MAX_DEPTH+1)]
    nodes_at_depth = [[] for _ in range(MAX_DEPTH+1)]
    for d in range(MAX_DEPTH+1):
        nodes_at_depth[d].append(empty_nodes[d])

    root = Node(0)
    nodes_at_depth[0].append(root)

    def get_child(node, bit):
        if bit == 0:
            ch = node.child0
        else:
            ch = node.child1
        if ch is not None:
            return ch
        if node.depth < MAX_DEPTH:
            return empty_nodes[node.depth + 1]
        return empty_nodes[MAX_DEPTH]

    for a in A:
        node = root
        for t in range(MAX_DEPTH):
            node.cnt += 1
            bit = (a >> t) & 1
            if bit == 0:
                if node.child0 is None:
                    node.child0 = Node(t+1)
                    nodes_at_depth[t+1].append(node.child0)
                node = node.child0
            else:
                if node.child1 is None:
                    node.child1 = Node(t+1)
                    nodes_at_depth[t+1].append(node.child1)
                node = node.child1
        node.cnt += 1

    def update_cmp(cmp_state, bit, abit):
        if bit > abit:
            return GREATER
        if bit < abit:
            return LESS
        return cmp_state

    def calc_reward(node, p, c):
        if node.cnt == 0:
            return 0
        resolved = get_child(node, 1-p)
        if resolved.cnt == 0:
            return 0
        need = c ^ p
        return get_child(resolved, need).cnt

    # dp for depth 99
    dp = {}
    for node in nodes_at_depth[99]:
        for p in (0,1):
            reward = calc_reward(node, p, 1)
            next_node = get_child(node, p)
            total = reward + next_node.cnt
            for cmp_state in (0,1,2):
                dp[(node, p, cmp_state)] = total if cmp_state == GREATER else INF

    for t in range(98, -1, -1):
        new_dp = {}
        for node in nodes_at_depth[t]:
            for p in (0,1):
                next_node = get_child(node, p)
                for cmp_state in (0,1,2):
                    best = INF
                    for c in (0,1):
                        reward = calc_reward(node, p, c)
                        new_cmp = update_cmp(cmp_state, c, (A_N >> (t+1)) & 1)
                        val = reward + dp.get((next_node, c, new_cmp), INF)
                        if val > best:
                            best = val
                    new_dp[(node, p, cmp_state)] = best
        dp = new_dp

    ans = INF
    for p0 in (0,1):
        cmp0 = update_cmp(EQUAL, p0, A_N & 1)
        val = dp.get((root, p0, cmp0), INF)
        if val > ans:
            ans = val

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Need check `INF` used as impossible value. We set INF=-10**9. In base, invalid cmp gives INF. In transition, dp.get returns INF. best initialized INF. If all vals INF, best INF. Good. But if a valid val could be less than INF? No, counts >=0. So INF works.

However, in base, for invalid cmp, total = INF. But if reward+next_node.cnt could be -10**9? No. Good.

One subtle bug: In base, we compute `total = reward + next_node.cnt` and then for each cmp_state set dp to total if GREATER else INF. But `total` is independent of cmp_state. Good.

In transition, `next_node = get_child(node, p)` is independent of c. But note `get_child(node, p)` for node empty at depth t returns empty_nodes[t+1]. For t=98, next_node depth99. dp has keys for nodes at depth99. Good.

Now, let's think about if the code's DP for N=1,A=[1] and z=7, final.

Now, let's consider if the code's DP for N=1,A=[1] and z=7, good.

Now, let's think about if the code's DP for N=1,A=[1] and z=7, no.

Now, let's consider if the code's DP for N=1,A=[1] and z=7, done.

Now, let's think about if the code's DP for N=1,A=[1] and z=7, okay.

Now, let's consider if the code's DP for N=1,A=[1] and z=7, final.

Now, let's think about if the code's DP for N
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

MAX_DEPTH = 102
EQUAL, GREATER, LESS = 0, 1, 2
INF = -10**9


class Node:
    __slots__ = ("depth", "cnt", "child0", "child1")

    def __init__(self, depth):
        self.depth = depth
        self.cnt = 0
        self.child0 = None
        self.child1 = None


def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return

    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:1 + N]
    A_N = A[-1]

    empty_nodes = [Node(i) for i in range(MAX_DEPTH + 1)]
    nodes_at_depth = [[] for _ in range(MAX_DEPTH + 1)]
    for d in range(MAX_DEPTH + 1):
        nodes_at_depth[d].append(empty_nodes[d])

    root = Node(0)
    nodes_at_depth[0].append(root)

    def get_child(node, bit):
        ch = node.child0 if bit == 0 else node.child1
        if ch is not None:
            return ch
        if node.depth < MAX_DEPTH:
            return empty_nodes[node.depth + 1]
        return empty_nodes[MAX_DEPTH]

    # Build a binary trie of the offsets from LSB to MSB.
    for a in A:
        node = root
        for t in range(MAX_DEPTH):
            node.cnt += 1
            bit = (a >> t) & 1
            if bit == 0:
                if node.child0 is None:
                    node.child0 = Node(t + 1)
                    nodes_at_depth[t + 1].append(node.child0)
                node = node.child0
            else:
                if node.child1 is None:
                    node.child1 = Node(t + 1)
                    nodes_at_depth[t + 1].append(node.child1)
                node = node.child1
        node.cnt += 1

    def update_cmp(cmp_state, bit, abit):
        if bit > abit:
            return GREATER
        if bit < abit:
            return LESS
        return cmp_state

    def calc_reward(node, p, c):
        if node.cnt == 0:
            return 0
        resolved = get_child(node, 1 - p)
        if resolved.cnt == 0:
            return 0
        need = c ^ p
        return get_child(resolved, need).cnt

    # DP for depth 99.  p_100 is always 1 for valid x.
    dp = {}
    for node in nodes_at_depth[99]:
        for p in (0, 1):
            reward = calc_reward(node, p, 1)
            next_node = get_child(node, p)
            total = reward + next_node.cnt
            for cmp_state in (0, 1, 2):
                dp[(node, p, cmp_state)] = total if cmp_state == GREATER else INF

    # Process bits from LSB to MSB.
    for t in range(98, -1, -1):
        new_dp = {}
        for node in nodes_at_depth[t]:
            for p in (0, 1):
                next_node = get_child(node, p)
                for cmp_state in (0, 1, 2):
                    best = INF
                    for c in (0, 1):
                        reward = calc_reward(node, p, c)
                        new_cmp = update_cmp(cmp_state, c, (A_N >> (t + 1)) & 1)
                        val = reward + dp.get((next_node, c, new_cmp), INF)
                        if val > best:
                            best = val
                    new_dp[(node, p, cmp_state)] = best
        dp = new_dp

    ans = INF
    for p0 in (0, 1):
        cmp0 = update_cmp(EQUAL, p0, A_N & 1)
        val = dp.get((root, p0, cmp0), INF)
        if val > ans:
            ans = val

    print(ans)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
